       7 +  vendor: z.string().nullable(),

      13 +      description: z.string().nullable(),

      17 +    }),                                                        
      18 +  ),                                                           
      19 +  subtotal: money.describe("Exactly as printed; may include tax

         + z.number().nullable(), amount: money })),                     
      21 +  adjustments: z

         +ey }))                                                         
      23 +    .describe("Rounding, discount, service charge, tip"),      
      24 +  total: money,                                                
      25 +  missing: z.array(z.object({ field: z.string(), reason: z.enum
         +(["absent", "illegible"]) })),                                 
      26 +});                                                            
      27 +                                                               
      28 +export type Receipt = z.infer<typeof Receipt>;

⏺ I ran step 0 on one receipt with both the cheap and the strong model pairs,
  and all four models accepted the image and returned output that matched the
  schema. I used the OPENROUTER_API_KEY from .env through tsx --env-file and
  never read the file.

  ┌───────────────────────────────┬───────┬─────────┬────────┐
  │             Model             │ Time  │  Cost   │ Result │
  ├───────────────────────────────┼───────┼─────────┼────────┤
  │ anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5    │ 14.2s │ $0.0047 │ OK     │
  ├───────────────────────────────┼───────┼─────────┼────────┤
  │ google/gemini-3.8-flash       │ 6.4s  │ $0.0045 │ OK     │
  ├───────────────────────────────┼───────┼─────────┼────────┤
  │ anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5   │ 8.5s  │ $0.0111 │ OK     │
  ├───────────────────────────────┼───────┼─────────┼────────┤
  │ google/gemini-3.1-pro-preview │ 6.3s  │ $0.0104 │ OK     │
  └───────────────────────────────┴───────┴─────────┴────────┘

  All four gave the same vendor (PERNIAGAAN ZHENG HUI), date (2018-02-09),
  currency (MYR, from "RM"), subtotal (411.50), 6% tax (24.69), total (436.20)
  and number of line items (6). Only the tax label text differed.

  Changes from the plan:
  - API: generateObject is deprecated in AI SDK v7 (ai@7.0.118). I used
    generateText with output: Output.object({ schema }) instead, which still
    returns a typed object.
  - Routing: I set OpenRouter's require_parameters: true, so requests only go to
    backends that support structured output.
  - Model choice: OpenRouter lists gemini-3.8-flash as the newest Flash model,
    so I used it for the cheap pair. The strong pair is Sonnet 5.5 and Gemini
    3.1 Pro preview. These are in src/config.ts and are easy to change.

  Worth knowing for later steps:
  - Missed rounding line: 411.50 + 24.69 = 436.19, but the receipt says 436.20.
    None of the four models picked up a rounding adjustment. The ±0.05 check in
    step 4 covers this, but expect to see it often.
  - Hidden reasoning tokens: Gemini Flash used 969 output tokens against Haiku's
    397, probably on reasoning. Its cost still matched Haiku's.
  - Haiku speed: Haiku was the slowest at 14s. That only matters if you run the
    45 receipts one after another rather than in parallel.

  New files: package.json (pnpm, ESM), src/config.ts, src/schema.ts (the full
  schema from the plan) and scripts/smoke.ts. Re-run the test with pnpm smoke 
  [image]. Nothing is committed yet.

  Step 1 is labelling the 15 receipts by hand, and you should do that before
  looking at any more model output.

⏺ I added a signed rounding: number | null field, separate from adjustments, and
  all 4 models now return rounding=0.01 on this receipt. It adds up exactly:
  411.50 + 24.69 + 0.01 = 436.20.

  - Tolerance: now ±0.01, only to absorb float errors. I updated the step 4
    check in PLAN.md to include rounding.
  - Discount: Haiku, Sonnet and Gemini Pro also picked up the printed Discount 
    0.00 line under adjustments; Gemini Flash left it out.
  - Other edits: Some of these files seem to have been changed at the same time
    as mine, since their wording differs from what I wrote. I kept those
    versions and only removed a duplicate rounding= print in scripts/smoke.ts.

⏺ I drafted labels for 15 receipts in labels/labels.csv, with columns
  file,vendor,date,total,set,notes. I took each value from the image only.

  - Selection: 10 random receipts (seed 4) and 5 that looked worst (low
    resolution, scan noise, faint print, very long). I left out the smoke-test
    image and the known duplicate.
  - Caveat: I'm a Claude model, so my labels may share mistakes with Haiku and
    Sonnet. They are not independent ground truth, which is why your check
    matters.

  Please check these:
  1. X51005268275: this is a credit note, and its printed numbers don't add up:
     263.02 − 0.20 + 15.78 = 278.60, but it prints 278.80. I labelled what is
     printed (278.80). The sum check should flag it, which makes it a good test
     case.
  2. Vendor names: I picked one name where the receipt shows two:
     - X51005230605: PETRON BKT LANJAN SB, not ALSERKAM ENTERPRISE
     - X51005444041: BENS INDEPENDENT GROCER SDN. BHD, not B.I.G.
     - X51005447844: RESTAURANT JIAWEI, not JIAWEI HOUSE
  3. Dates: I read all dates as dd/mm. Most receipts give no way to confirm
     this, for example 02/01/2019 and 01/02/2018.

       1 +// Builds labels/review.html: each label row next to its receip

       2 +import { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";         
       3 +                                                               
       4 +function parseCsv(text: string): string[][] {                  
       5 +  const rows: string[][] = [];                                 
       6 +  let row: string[] = [], cell = "", quoted = false;           
       7 +  for (let i = 0; i < text.length; i++) {                      
       8 +    const c = text[i];                                         
       9 +    if (quoted) {                                              
      10 +      if (c === '"' && text[i + 1] === '"') cell += '"', i++;  
      11 +      else if (c === '"') quoted = false;                      
      12 +      else cell += c;                                          
      13 +    } else if (c === '"') quoted = true;                       
      14 +    else if (c === ",") row.push(cell), (cell = "");           
      15 +    else if (c === "\n") row.push(cell), rows.push(row), (row =
         + []), (cell = "");                                             
      16 +    else if (c !== "\r") cell += c;                            
      17 +  }                                                            
      18 +  if (cell || row.length) row.push(cell), rows.push(row);      
      19 +  return rows;                                                 
      20 +}                                                              
      21 +                                                               
      22 +const esc = (s: string) => s.replace(/[&<>"]/g, (c) => `&${{ "&
         +": "amp", "<": "lt", ">": "gt", '"': "quot" }[c]};`);

⏺ You were right. I had picked 469671 and 288570 as "worst" by file size and
  image size without looking at them, and they're clean. I've removed them and
  added two receipts I actually looked at:

  - X51005268408, 99 SPEED MART S/B: faded dot-matrix print, punch holes, and a
    confusing total line. The final amount 169.80 is printed next to the label
    "Rounding RM", and a separate rounding adjustment of .02 is printed above
    it.
  - X51005337867, Old Town Kopitiam Sdn Bhd: a "THIS IS NOT A RECEIPT" guest
    check. It has items without prices, a service charge, rounding of 0.01, torn
    edges and handwriting. By your vendor rule the vendor is the legal name,
    not OLDTOWN WHITE COFFEE.

  I also looked at 361906 and 568866, but they're fairly clean, so I left them
  out. review.html is rebuilt; refresh the page to see the changes.

⏺ Step 2 is done. On the 5 labelled receipts, Haiku now matches my labels for
  vendor, date and total on all 5.

  Prompt fixes found in this step:
  1. Vendor rule: the prompt now says the name next to the company number may be
     a legal "… Sdn Bhd" name. That fixed 337867.
  2. missing: now limited to top-level fields. Set items with no printed price
     get null instead of being marked "illegible".
  3. adjustments: now excludes discounts already listed as line items, summary
     lines like "Total Savings", and payment lines like cash, card and change.
     Before this, 568855 was counted twice and then picked up "Mastercard
     −140.65".

  Other changes:
  - Cache: each result in cache/<model>/<file>.json stores a hash of the prompt
    and schema, so editing the prompt re-runs the extraction instead of
    returning stale results.
  - Typecheck: added tsconfig.json; pnpm typecheck passes.

  For the failure log: 268408 (faded dot-matrix) gave subtotal 169.78 on the
  earlier runs and 169.76 on the last run, even at temperature 0. The sum check
  should catch it, because 169.76 + 0.02 ≠ 169.80.

  Each receipt costs about $0.005 and takes 7–16s with Haiku. Nothing is
  committed yet.

  Next is step 3: add Gemini as the second reader, and run the cheap pair
  against the strong pair on the 15 labelled receipts.

✻ Cogitated for 2m 11s · done 10:23 AM

⏺ I ran all 4 models on the 15 labelled receipts. The cheap pair is as accurate
  as the strong pair and costs about a third as much, so I'd ship cheap.

  ┌──────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────┐
  │      Model       │ Vendor, date and total all correct │ Cost for 15 │
  ├──────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ Haiku 4.5        │ 14/15                              │ $0.067      │
  ├──────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ Gemini 3.8 Flash │ 15/15                              │ $0.132      │
  ├──────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ Sonnet 5.5       │ 13/15 (vendor rule)                │ $0.180      │
  ├──────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ Gemini 3.1 Pro   │ 15/15                              │ $0.476      │
  └──────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────┘

  In both pairs, every error the primary model made showed up as a disagreement
  on vendor, date or total, and there were no false flags on those fields.
  That's a small, in-sample result.

  What step 4 needs to handle:
  1. Currency: Flash returns null when only "RM" is printed. The code should
     work out the currency from currency_symbol_seen instead of asking the
     model.
  2. Subtotal: when no subtotal line is printed, Haiku copies the total into it.
     Flash correctly returns null. The "absent subtotal" rule covers this.
  3. Line items: the item sums disagree on 5 of 15 receipts. Under the current
     rules these receipts would all drop to MEDIUM.

  Decision for you: Flash beat Haiku on accuracy here (15 vs 14), so it could be
  the primary instead. The difference is one receipt, so I've left the order as
  it is.

  I also started NOTES.md with the tier result and the failure log so far, and
  fixed float noise in the item sums.

         +v) ? v.join("; ") : String(v);

  ⎿  Created src/dedupe.ts (+14 -0)
       1 +import { createHash } from "node:crypto";                      
       2 +                                                               
       3 +// Exact byte duplicates: maps each later copy (in sorted file 
         +order) to the first.                                           
       4 +export function findDuplicates(files: { file: string; bytes: Bu
         +ffer }[]): Map<string, string> {                               
       5 +  const first = new Map<string, string>();                     
       6 +  const dupes = new Map<string, string>();                     
       7 +  for (const { file, bytes } of [...files].sort((x, y) => x.fil
         +e.localeCompare(y.file))) {                                    
       8 +    const hash = createHash("sha256").update(bytes).digest("hex
         +");                                                            
       9 +    const seen = first.get(hash);                              
      10 +    if (seen) dupes.set(file, seen);                           
      11 +    else first.set(hash, file);                                
      12 +  }                                                            
      13 +  return dupes;                                                
      14 +}

       96      source,

       98  }
       99 +                                                              
      100 +// A failed model call never drops a receipt: score what we ha
          +ve and send it to review.                                     
      101 +export function scoreReadings(file: string, a: Receipt | Error
          +, b: Receipt | Error, today = new Date()): Row {              
      102 +  const failed = [["A", a], ["B", b]].flatMap(([n, r]) => (r i
          +nstanceof Error ? [`model ${n} failed: ${r.message}`] : [])); 
      103 +  if (!failed.length) return score(file, a as Receipt, b as Re
          +ceipt, today);                                                
      104 +  const ok = [a, b].find((r): r is Receipt => !(r instanceof E
          +rror));                                                       
      105 +  if (!ok) return emptyRow(file, "LOW", failed);              
      106 +  const row = score(file, ok, ok, today);                     
      107 +  const status = row.status === "UNREADABLE" ? row.status : "L
          +OW";                                                          
      108 +  return { ...row, status, confidence: STATUSES.indexOf(status
          +), reasons: [...failed, ...row.reasons], source: ok === a ? "A
          +" : "B" };                                                    
      109 +}

  ⎿  Created src/pool.ts (+16 -0)
       1 +// Run fn over items with at most n in flight; results keep inp
         +ut order.                                                      
       2 +export async function pool<T, R>(items: T[], n: number, fn: (x:
         + T) => Promise<R>): Promise<PromiseSettledResult<R>[]> {       
       3 +  const out: PromiseSettledResult<R>[] = new Array(items.length
         +);                                                             
       4 +  let next = 0;                                                
       5 +  const worker = async () => {                                 
       6 +    while (next < items.length) {                              
       7 +      const i = next++;                                        
       8 +      out[i] = await fn(items[i]).then(                        
       9 +        (value) => ({ status: "fulfilled" as const, value }),  
      10 +        (reason) => ({ status: "rejected" as const, reason }), 
      11 +      );                                                       
      12 +    }                                                          
      13 +  };                                                           
      14 +  await Promise.all(Array.from({ length: n }, worker));        
      15 +  return out;                                                  
      16 +}

⏺ The full pipeline ran on all 45 receipts (2 LOW, 14 MEDIUM, 28 HIGH, 1
  DUPLICATE), but I found two bugs: line items sometimes get pulled from the
  wrong model when totals match, and vendor names with registration numbers
  appended are wrongly flagged as disagreements. I'll write tests for both
  before fixing them.

         +d"] });                                                        
      23 +  assert.deepEqual(r.signals, { date: { fired: 1, falseFlags: 0
         + }, items: { fired: 1, falseFlags: 1 } });                     
      24 +});

       1 +# Receipt extraction with validation                           
       2 +                                                               
       3 +Reads receipt images and writes a CSV where every row says how 
         +far to trust it, and what to check when it can't.              
       4 +                                                               
       5 +A fixed pipeline, not an agent loop: the model reads, code deci
         +des trust.

       7 +```

         +ut/line_items.csv

  ⎿  Created prompts.md (+36 -0)
       1 +# Prompts                                                      
       2 +                                                               
       3 +One prompt, sent with the image to both models (`src/extract.ts
         +`), temperature 0.                                             
       4 +Output is forced to the Zod schema in `src/schema.ts` via struc
         +tured output (`Output.object`).                                
       5 +                                                               
       6 +```text                                                        
       7 +Extract the data printed on this receipt.                      
       8 +                                                               
       9 +Rules:                                                         
      10 +- Copy values exactly as printed. Never guess, never compute a 
         +value that is not printed.                                     
      11 +- If a field is printed but you cannot read it, return null and
         + add it to "missing" with reason "illegible".                  
      12 +- If a field is not printed, return null and add it to "missing
         +" with reason "absent".                                        
      13 +- "missing" covers only the top-level fields listed in its sche
         +ma, not line items.                                            
      14 +- vendor: the business name printed on the same line as, or dir
         +ectly above, the company registration number (e.g. "(123456-X)"
         +, "Co. No."). This may be a legal name like "... Sdn Bhd" rathe
         +r than the brand name at the top. If no registration number is 
         +printed, the name at the top.                                  
      15 +- date: the transaction date as yyyy-mm-dd. Printed dates are d
         +ay-first (dd/mm/yy or dd/mm/yyyy).                             
      16 +- line_items: one entry per product. A discount printed under a
         +n item is its own entry with a negative amount. An item with no
         + printed price (e.g. part of a set) gets null unit_price and am
         +ount.                                                          
      17 +- subtotal: the printed subtotal line, as printed (it may inclu
         +de tax). Null if not printed.                                  
      18 +- taxes: each tax amount printed (e.g. GST, SST). Include it ev
         +en if the receipt says it is already included in the total.    
      19 +- adjustments: bill-level discounts (negative), service charge,
         + tip. Not rounding. Not discounts already listed as line items.
         + Not summary lines such as "Total Savings". Not payment lines (
         +cash, card, change).                                           
      20 +- rounding: the printed rounding adjustment, signed. Null if no
         +t printed.                                                     
      21 +- total: the final amount payable, after rounding. Not cash ten
         +dered, not change.                                             
      22 +- legible: false only if the receipt as a whole cannot be read.
      23 +```                                                            
      24 +                                                               
      25 +## Why each rule is there                                      
      26 +                                                               
      27 +| Rule | Found on |                                            
      28 +|---|---|                                                      
      29 +| vendor = name next to the registration number | brand vs lega
         +l name (OLDTOWN WHITE COFFEE vs Old Town Kopitiam Sdn Bhd) |   
      30 +| "missing" only for top-level fields | set items without price
         +s were marked illegible, which would downgrade clean receipts |
      31 +| adjustments: not item discounts, not "Total Savings" | Popula
         +r Book receipt: discounts counted twice |                      
      32 +| adjustments: not payment lines | "Mastercard -140.65" read as
         + an adjustment |                                               
      33 +| rounding as its own field | printed "Rounding 0.01" was dropp
         +ed; the sum check needs it |                                   
      34 +| total: after rounding, not cash or change | receipts print CA
         +SH and CHANGE in the same bold style |                         
      35 +                                                               
      36 +Changing the prompt or schema changes its hash, so cached resul
         +ts are re-run (`cache/<model>/<file>.json`).

› Message from a0f9cc606cb6be3bc
  ## Review: receipt extraction pipeline, b2adcb1..HEAD (b29d3ed)

  Verified: `pnpm test` 52/52 pass, `pnpm typecheck` clean. I read every src/,
  test/, and scripts/ file, PLAN.md, the progress rulings, NOTES.md,
  out/receipts.csv, and probed hypotheses with a scratch script
  (`/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-devdalia-personal-workspace-S4/ebf484ac-f9f8-
  4b48-ab94-b74c2d93a418/scratchpad/probe.ts`) against fixtures and real cached
  readings. No API calls, no writes to the repo.

  ### Strengths
  - Architecture matches the plan exactly: fixed pipeline, model reads, code
  decides trust. Every module is small and single-purpose; `score.ts` is the
  only place status is decided.
  - The "sums pass, else primary" source selection (`src/score.ts:50`) plus the
  separate line-items source (`:54`) is a good call and is backed by real cases
  (230621, 568855, 442366).
  - No receipt is ever dropped: model failure → LOW with the survivor's values
  (`scoreReadings`); `res.output` on a no-output response throws
  `NoOutputGeneratedError` (checked in ai@7.0.118), which `pool` catches, so no
  crash path there.
  - Cache keyed on prompt+schema hash (`src/extract.ts:29`) means stale readings
  are never silently reused.
  - Reasons carry both models' values and which was used, as the plan asks.
  Vendor normalization dropping bracketed registration numbers removed real
  false MEDIUMs.
  - Tests exercise real behavior (status transitions, source selection, both
  failure modes, date validity, currency derivation), not just shape. The tuning
  pass is recorded with its cost-if-wrong.
  - Rulings in progress.md are sensible and each records a fallback. Dropping
  the printed-rate check (fired on 8/15) was right.

  ### Issues

  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  None.

  #### Important (Should Fix)

  **1. A currency disagreement between the two models never downgrades, so a
  wrong currency can ship at HIGH.**
  `src/score.ts:61` skips `currency` in the compare loop and `:70` emits only a
  reason. Real case: `cache/.../X51005433543.jpg` — Haiku says `SGD` (symbol
  "$"), Flash says `MYR`; the receipt is UNIHAKKA INTERNATIONAL (Malaysian, MYR
  on its four sibling receipts). Today the row is MEDIUM only because Haiku also
  misspelled the vendor ("SDN EHD"). With B's vendor set equal to A's,
  `score()` returns **HIGH, currency SGD** (probe (a)). The plan lists currency
  under compare and is silent on its scoring; a reasonable user treats a
  two-reader disagreement on the currency of a money column as at least MEDIUM.
  Fix: in the `:70` branch, `medium.add("currency")`. Optional refinement: when
  exactly one reading's currency is symbol-backed (`SYMBOLS` hit) prefer it over
  an inferred one; here neither is ("$" is unmapped), so the downgrade is the
  essential part. One test: `score(receipt({currency:"SGD",
  currency_symbol_seen:"$"}), receipt({currency:"MYR",
  currency_symbol_seen:"$"}))` should be MEDIUM.

  **2. `passes()` treats "total check skipped + items add up" as a full pass,
  which (a) can drop a validated total and (b) prints "(sums match)" when no sum
  was checked.**
  `src/score.ts:33-34`. Probe (b): A `total: null` (illegible) with items =
  printed subtotal; B complete and passing tax-added. Result: source A, CSV
  `total` empty, reason `check total: A=none B=436.20, used A (sums match)`.
  Status is LOW (correct), but the plan says the CSV gets "the value that passes
  the arithmetic checks" — B's 436.20 did, A's null passed nothing — and the
  reason asserts a sum match that never ran. Same wording defect on a real row,
  `X51005442366.jpg`: `check subtotal: A=22.60 B=none, used B (sums match)` —
  B's subtotal is null, its total check was `skipped`; what actually matched was
  items = total. Fix: rank readings instead of booleans — `2` for
  tax-added/tax-inclusive, `1` for skipped-but-items-back-it, `0` otherwise;
  choose B only when strictly higher; and make `why` say "(items add up)" when
  `totalCheck === "skipped"`. Two small tests cover both.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)

  **3. "items add up" is claimed for a reading with no item amounts, and its
  empty list beats the other reading's validated one.** `src/score.ts:53`
  `itemsOk = itemsCheck !== "fail"` counts `skipped` as ok. Probe (e): A's items
  all `amount: null`, B's items sum to subtotal → `itemsSource A`, reason
  `check line items: A=0.00 B=411.50, used A (items add up)`, and line_items.csv
  gets A's null-amount rows. No shipped cache file hits this, so it's
  hypothetical. Fix: prefer the other reading when source is `skipped` and the
  other is `subtotal`/`total`.

  **4. Zero-vs-none noise in reasons.** `src/compare.ts:14-15` `sum([])` is
  `null` but `sum([{amount:0}])` is `0`, so `check adjustments: A=none B=0.00,
  used A (sums match)` appears on 3 real HIGH rows (200931, 447844, 568890) and
  would for `tax` too. These are not disagreements a reviewer needs to check.
  Fix: in `sameValue`, or in `fieldValues`, treat `null` and `0` as equal for
  the summed fields.

  **5. Missing/invalid API key with a cache miss produces a "successful" run of
  all-LOW rows.** `src/index.ts` exits 0 and writes 45 rows reading `model A
  failed: ...`. Reasons are explicit, so not a trust-signal bug, but a config
  error should fail loudly. Fix: if every settled job rejected,
  `process.exit(1)` with the first message.

  **6. `scripts/eval.ts:38` cost averages read every file in the model's cache
  dir regardless of prompt hash**, so stale-prompt runs (there were several
  prompt iterations per NOTES.md) skew the reported per-call cost. Filter on
  `e.prompt === PROMPT_HASH` (export it from extract.ts).

  **7. Cosmetic:** `X51005442322` vendor column keeps "(289560-H)" because the
  row uses the raw string while compare strips brackets. Fine per plan ("copy as
  printed"); mention only so nobody thinks it's a bug.

  ### Declined to judge
  - UNREADABLE when one model says `legible:false` discards the other model's
  values entirely — plan step 5 mandates "either model" and "values left empty".
  - Vendor column is always the primary's on disagreement (361912: A "TED HENG"
  wrong, B "TEO HENG" right, row shows A) — plan step 3 says "else the
  primary's"; MEDIUM flag is present.
  - Tax-inclusive sum form lets "subtotal copied from total" pass unflagged
  (NOTES: Haiku does this) — plan's known-limits list covers it.
  - Line-item disagreement no longer MEDIUM by itself — the plan's one allowed
  tuning pass, recorded with numbers.
  - Printed-rate mismatch check dropped — ruling; plan made it reason-only
  anyway.
  - Dedupe is exact-bytes only — plan step 1 says exact duplicate.
  - DUPLICATE rows carry no values — plan.
  - Reasons from the non-source reading's `validate()` (e.g. B's "date in the
  future") are not merged — a date disagreement already forces LOW, so no trust
  effect.
  - `out/` committed, `cache/` ignored — rulings.
  - No `is_receipt` guard, no PDF/multi-page — plan "Later".
  - README / prompts.md missing — told not to flag.

  ### Recommendations

  a trust label or a reason text can be wrong on a real receipt.
  - Fold 3 and 4 in if touching `score.ts` anyway; they're each a one-liner.
  - Add the 433543 pair as a regression fixture in `test/score.test.ts` — it's
  the one real receipt in the set where the second reader caught something the
  sums could not.

  ### Assessment

  plan; but a two-model currency disagreement can currently sit at HIGH on a
  real receipt, and the "(sums match)" reason is printed in a case where no sum

⏺ The implementation is done and reviewed: all 55 tests pass, the pipeline gives
  LOW 2, MEDIUM 3, HIGH 39, DUPLICATE 1, and nothing is pushed. The fresh
  reviewer said "ready with fixes"; I made its two Important fixes, and it found
  nothing Critical.

  Rulings I made:
  - Tasks: the build-order rows served as tasks, since PLAN.md has no Task N
    headings.
  - Test runner: node:test through tsx, so no new dependency.
  - Tax rate: I dropped the printed tax-rate check. It fired on 8 of 15
    receipts, all noise. Cost if wrong: about 6 lines to add back.
  - Currency: taken from the printed symbol, e.g. RM → MYR, not from the model's
    currency code. An unrecognised symbol counts as "inferred". Cost if wrong:
    a bad entry in the symbol map.
  - Subtotal: a null subtotal is computed as total − tax. If it's marked
    illegible, it isn't computed and the total check is skipped.
  - Which reading's numbers are used: one reading supplies the numbers for the
    whole receipt, ranked "sums add up" first, then "items back it", then the
    primary. Line items use their own source by the same idea.
  - Downgrades:
    - An invalid or future date, or an illegible total or date, gives LOW.
    - A currency disagreement gives MEDIUM (this came from the review).
    - Subtotal, tax, adjustments and rounding disagreements are reasons only.
  - Failed model calls: a failed call gives LOW with the other model's values,
    so no receipt is dropped.
  - Vendor matching: bracketed registration numbers are ignored when comparing
    vendors.
  - Git: out/ is committed; cache/ is ignored, as you asked.
  - Tuning pass: a line-item disagreement is MEDIUM only if the items I used
    don't add up. This moved MEDIUM from 12 to 3. Cost if wrong: a real
    item-list difference is hidden when one reading happens to add up, though
    the reason stays in the CSV.
  - Reviewer's set-aside items: all follow PLAN.md or a ruling above.

  Deferred minors:
  - The line-item source treats "no amounts at all" as OK. This is hypothetical;
    nothing in the current 45 receipts hits it.
  - Null vs 0 shows up as a disagreement in reasons, for example "adjustments
    A=none B=0.00" on 3 HIGH rows.
  - A missing or invalid API key exits with success and 45 LOW rows instead of
    failing loudly.
  - The eval's cost averages include cache files from older prompts.
  - The vendor column keeps the bracketed registration number as printed.

  The docs are still deferred.

  Implementation complete. What would you like to do?

  1. Merge back to main locally
  2. Push and create a Pull Request
  3. Keep the branch as-is (I'll handle it later)

  what do you thinkk is our fundamental problem here?                           

⏺ You're right, and the two rows aren't separate bugs. The core problem is that
  I treated "the numbers add up" as evidence the numbers are real.

  The sum check only proves a reading is internally consistent. A model that
  fills in values it can't see will usually fill them in so they add up: it
  applies the same arithmetic our validator checks. So the check isn't
  independent of the model inventing values. It rewards a complete, tidy
  reading, and that's exactly what an invented one looks like.

  447844 shows the whole problem:
  - Flash was honest: it returned null for the three amounts cut off at the
    edge.
  - Haiku made them up: it copied them from the unit prices (20, 18, 16), which
    breaks the prompt's "never compute" rule.
  - The scoring rewarded Haiku: Flash's items "don't add up", Haiku's do, so we
    used Haiku's and marked the row HIGH.

  Three of my decisions made this worse:
  1. The tuning pass: I removed the MEDIUM for item disagreement because the
     eval called those flags "false". But the labels only cover vendor, date and
     total, so the eval couldn't see whether the items were right. I tuned
     against a metric that can't measure what I was changing.
  2. The missing change: I limited missing to top-level fields. That removed the
     models' only way to say "I can't see this item amount".
  3. Adjustments as reason only: that's how 268275 stays HIGH even though one
     model saw a discount and the other didn't.

  The only independent signal we have is two readers disagreeing. Arithmetic
  should choose which value goes in the CSV, but it should never decide the
  status.

  The fix:
  - Status: any disagreement on a money field (subtotal, tax, adjustments,
    rounding, line items) is at least MEDIUM, whichever reading adds up. Total
    and date stay LOW.
  - Output: keep using the reading whose sums add up for the CSV values.
  - Rule vs noise: "none" vs 0.00 is not a disagreement, so the rule doesn't
    bring the noise back.
  - Docs: undo the tuning ruling and record it in NOTES.md under "tried, didn't
    work", since it's a better story for the Loom than the one I had.

  MEDIUM will go back up to about 12 rows. That's more honest, because on those
  rows the two readers really do see different numbers.

  Should I go ahead? I'd keep it to those changes plus the tests for them.

✻ Cooked for 17s · done 11:34 AM

❯ Please give me very short answer on this                                      

⏺ The fundamental problem is that I treated "the sums add up" as proof the
  numbers are real. A model that invents values makes them add up, so arithmetic
  can't catch invented values. I then let arithmetic override the one
  independent signal we have, which is the two models disagreeing.

  The fix: any disagreement on a money field makes the row at least MEDIUM.
  Arithmetic only picks which value goes in the CSV, never the status.

  Should I go ahead?

✻ Brewed for 4s · done 11:36 AM

❯ but the core problem is haiku's made up value?                                

⏺ Haiku inventing the values is what caused the error, but it isn't what we can
  fully fix. Every model invents sometimes; the plan itself says "perfect
  extraction is not the goal". What's broken is that our scoring hid it: the
  models disagreed, and we still said HIGH.

  So we should do both:
  1. Catch it: any disagreement on a money field makes the row MEDIUM. This is
     the must-fix.
  2. Invent less: Flash left the unreadable amounts as null while Haiku made
     them up, and Flash also scored 15/15 against Haiku's 14/15. We could make
     Flash the primary. That would reduce invented values, but can't stop them.

✻ Sautéed for 6s · done 11:36 AM

※ recap: We're building the receipt extraction pipeline that flags receipts it might have wrong; right now two receipts with invented amounts were wrongly marked HIGH. Next step: your go-ahead to make any money-field disagreement MEDIUM, and optionally make Flash the primary model.

❯ Ok and model curently has a way to say "cant see" right?                      

⏺ Only partly:
  - Top-level fields: yes. missing lets the model mark total, date, subtotal and
    the others as illegible.
  - Line items: no. I limited missing to top-level fields. A model can return
    amount: null, and Flash did, but that doesn't say whether the price wasn't
    printed or couldn't be read.
  - Scoring: a null amount makes the items "not add up", so the honest reading
    loses to the invented one.

  The fix is an illegible flag on each line item, which our code treats as
  "cannot see" and flags as MEDIUM, never as the worse reading.

✻ Crunched for 6s · done 11:44 AM

❯ Are we running both models parallely or sequentially?                         

⏺ In parallel. src/index.ts queues one call per receipt per model and runs up to
  8 at a time (pool(jobs, 8, …)). A receipt's two calls sit next to each other
  in the queue, so they usually run at the same time, together with other
  receipts' calls.

✻ Cooked for 2m 54s · done 11:55 AM

❯ what is this thing related to primary?                                        

⏺ The primary is model A, currently Haiku. It only matters when the two models
  disagree:
  - Vendor and date: always taken from the primary.
  - Numbers: taken from whichever reading's sums add up. If neither adds up,
    from the primary.

  It doesn't change the status. A disagreement is flagged either way.

  Why Flash might be the better primary:
  - Accuracy: it beat Haiku on the tier test, 15/15 against 14/15.
  - Honesty: on 447844 it left the unreadable amounts null, while Haiku made
    them up.

  So on flagged rows, the value shown would more often be right.

  It costs nothing to try. The cache is stored per model, so swapping them in
  src/config.ts needs no new API calls, only the full run you're already
  planning.

✻ Brewed for 7s · done 11:56 AM

※ recap: We're building the receipt extractor that flags when it might be wrong, and I've just made model disagreements on money fields flag the row and tightened the subtotal prompt. Next: you choose whether Flash becomes the primary, then I run the full extraction.

❯ I feel we shouldn't have this primary thing                                   
  what we can do is , if both models agree, we fill value                       
  if they disagree, we can leave cell empty instead, no?                        
  and reduce confidence or is this not good?                                    

⏺ Yes, I think that's better, and it's simpler.

  Why it works:
  - No hidden choice: a value only appears when both models read it the same
    way, so the CSV never shows a value one model may have made up.
  - Nothing is lost: the reason keeps both readings, e.g. check total: A=436.20 
    B=437.20, so a reviewer still sees them.
  - The status already drops: LOW or MEDIUM, as now.
  - Less code: the primary, the reading-ranking logic and "used A (sums match)"
    all go away.

  What it costs:
  - More empty cells: about 25 of the 45 rows would have at least one blank.
    That's honest: someone has to check them anyway.
  - We lose the arithmetic tiebreak: we can keep it as a hint in the reason,
    e.g. A=436.20 B=437.20 (only A adds up). It helps the reviewer but never
    fills the cell.

  Two details to decide:
  1. Line items: if the models' item totals disagree, write no items for that
     receipt rather than one model's list.
  2. One model failed: there's nothing to agree with, so fill from the model
     that answered and mark the row LOW, as now.

  Should I implement it that way?

✻ Crunched for 11s · done 12:01 PM

❯ mental model be like: value only trusted when 2 models agree. code should not 
  break the tie and it reports disagreement?                                    

⏺ Yes, with one addition: arithmetic still checks the agreed values. Two models
  can agree on a wrong number, so if agreed values don't add up, the row is LOW.

  So the whole model is:
  - Both agree: fill the value, then check the sums.
  - They disagree: leave the cell empty, report both values, and lower the
    status. Code never breaks the tie.

  To stay consistent, if one model fails there's nothing to agree with, so those
  cells should be empty too, not filled from the survivor.

✻ Worked for 6s · done 12:03 PM

❯ not every disagreement would mean unreadable though                           
  if one model says null and other gives value..that's a guess then correct?    
  if both reads different would be a misread                                    
  amd if they read it differently, maybe it could be prompt definition problem  
  Should we fix definitions, then maybe label the disagreement type in reason   
  Or should we force the model to give evidence in the output: eg: {  value:    
  "x", evidence: "total sales X" } i.e. evidence from actual invoice text       
  right?                                                                        
  and maybe this would be good for reevieweer then at last so that he can know  
  what to check                                                                 
  it might also catch invented value maybe                                      
  what do you think?                                                            

⏺ Mostly yes, with one correction and one limit.

  Correction: null against a value isn't always a guess. The model that said
  null may have missed a line that's really printed. So it's one-sided, and we
  can't tell which model is wrong.

  The three disagreement types:

  ┌─────────────┬──────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┐
  │    Type     │        Looks like        │         Likely cause          │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ One-sided   │ A=30.68, B=none          │ one model invented a value,   │
  │             │                          │ or the other missed it        │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ Misread     │ same printed line,       │ bad image or digit confusion  │
  │             │ different number         │                               │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ Different   │ each model quotes a      │ the prompt definition is      │
  │ line        │ different printed line   │ unclear (the subtotal case)   │
  └─────────────┴──────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┘

  Evidence: I think it's worth doing. Each top-level field returns the printed
  text it came from, e.g. { value: 30.68, evidence: "Total Sales (Excluding GST)
  : 30.68" }. Then:
  - Code labels the disagreement type: same label with a different number is a
    misread, different labels means different lines, and a missing quote is
    one-sided. The model never classifies it.
  - Code checks the value appears in the quote. A value calculated from qty ×
    price has no printed line to quote, so this catches some invented values.
  - Reviewer: sees exactly which line to look at.
  - Prompt definitions: "different line" rows tell us which definitions to fix.

  Limit: a model can invent the quote too. Evidence makes inventing harder and
  easier to spot, but it doesn't prove the value is real. Only OCR or a person
  looking at the image can do that.

  Scope, to avoid overengineering:
  - Fields: evidence only on vendor, date, subtotal, taxes, adjustments,
    rounding and total. For line items, each item is already its own printed
    line.
  - Cost: about 30% more output tokens.
  - Run: one full extraction run after the change.

  Should I plan it that way, together with the "empty cell when models disagree"
  change, and then do a single full run?
✻ Cooked for 15s · done 12:10 PM

❯ Also, on the reviewer's end, we don't want to show model-related texts like   
  "Haiku gave this response" and "Flash gave this response," something like     
  that, right? because it's user-facing.                                        

⏺ Right. The reviewer shouldn't need to know how many models there are or which
  is which. Even "A" and "B" leak that. Reasons should talk about the receipt:

  ┌──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │         Now          │                   User-facing                    │
  ├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ check total:         │ check total: read as 436.20 or 437.20 ("Total    │
  │ A=436.20 B=437.20    │ (RM): 436.20")                                   │
  ├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ check subtotal:      │ check subtotal: 30.68 may not be printed ("Total │
  │ A=30.68 B=none       │  Sales (Excluding GST): 30.68")                  │
  ├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ model B failed:      │ could not be read automatically, check all       │
  │ timeout              │ fields                                           │
  └──────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  The model names and per-model values would go to a separate debug file,
  out/debug.jsonl, for us and the Loom, never into receipts.csv.

  I'll include this in the plan with evidence and empty cells on disagreement.
  Should I write the plan now?

⏺ All 57 tests pass and the typecheck is clean. Committing:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The changes are committed as 393c1fb and all 57 tests pass. out/ hasn't been
  regenerated yet.

  What changed:
  - No primary: a value is filled only when both models agree. On a disagreement
    the cell is left empty and the reason reports it. If one model's call
    fails, every cell is empty and the row is LOW.
  - Disagreement types in reasons:
    - read as X or Y, when both quote the same printed line.
    - X ("…") or Y ("…"), different printed lines, when each quotes a different
      line.
    - X may not be printed ("…"), when only one model found a value.
  - Evidence: each field now carries the quoted printed line. If a number
    doesn't appear in its own quote, the row is flagged as possibly made up.
  - User-facing text: reasons never mention models. The per-model readings go to
    out/debug.jsonl.
  - Line items: written only when both models agree and every amount was
    readable.
  - Eval: each field is now counted as right, empty or filled-but-wrong.
    Filled-but-wrong is the number that has to stay at 0.

  Ready for the full run. The schema changed, so all 88 calls run again. It
  should cost about $0.70, since the evidence quotes add output tokens. Should I
  run pnpm start and pnpm eval?

✻ Cogitated for 4m 11s · done 12:18 PM

※ recap: We're building a receipt extractor that only trusts values both models agree on and flags the rest. That change is committed with all tests passing, and next is the full re-run and eval (about $0.70), waiting on your go-ahead.

❯ - Do we still show invented amounts in line items.csv?                        
  - for csv, should we add coloring, sorting or grouping?                       
  - is unreadable wqorking no receipt is marked yet, right?                     

⏺ 1. Invented amounts in line_items.csv: less likely now, but still possible.
     - Blocked: items are written only when both models' item totals agree and
       no amount is marked unreadable, so the 447844 case no longer gets
       through.
     - Still possible: both models invent the same number, such as both
       computing qty × price. There's also a gap: we compare only the item
       totals, not each line, so two different lists with the same total pass.
       Items have no evidence check either.
     - Fix: compare the items line by line instead of just the total. It's
       small, but it will flag more receipts.
  2. Coloring, sorting, grouping: a CSV can't hold colors. Rows are already
     sorted worst first, which groups them by status. If you want color, the
     right place is a small HTML review page like the labels one, listed under
     "Later" in the plan. I'd keep the CSV as plain data.
  3. UNREADABLE: right, no receipt has been marked UNREADABLE. None of the 45
     are that bad, so the rule has only been checked by 2 unit tests, never on a
     real image. We could test it with a deliberately blurred or cropped copy
     of one receipt: 2 calls, about $0.02.

  Which of these should I do before the full run: the line-by-line item
  comparison, the UNREADABLE test, or both?

     came out UNREADABLE with every value empty and the reason "receipt could
     not be read".

  That test also found a bug that would have broken the full run. With the
  evidence fields, the schema had 23 nullable fields, and OpenRouter's Azure
  backend for Haiku rejects anything over 16. Every Haiku call would have
  failed. I changed evidence to a list, which brings it to exactly 16, and added
  a test that fails if a future change goes over the limit.

⏺ Yes, I'd add it. Reviewing with the image next to its reasons is the whole
  job, and a CSV makes you open each image separately.

  What I'd build:
  - One file: a static out/review.html, written on every run from the same rows
    as the CSV, with no extra logic.
  - One card per receipt, sorted worst first like the CSV. Each card has:
    - a colored status badge (UNREADABLE, LOW, MEDIUM, HIGH, DUPLICATE)
    - an image thumbnail you click to open full size
    - the filled values, with the empty ones clearly marked "check"
    - the reasons, so you know what to look at on the image
    - the line items in a collapsed section, when both models agreed on them
  - Status filter buttons at the top, with a few lines of plain JS and no
    framework.

  I'd keep the CSV as the real output. The HTML is only for looking at it.

  It stays a local file, not a hosted page, because the receipts come from the
  private brief. It's about 60 lines, reusing the pattern from the labels review
  page.

  Should I add it before the full run?
